# GRH: Riemann hypothesis for Dirichlet L-functions: the route of the shorter formal proof
<!-- tauceti-targets:v1 -->
Goal: the Riemann hypothesis for Dirichlet L-functions, the end theorems of the roadmap family
ShortGRH: for every Dirichlet character χ and every s with Re s > 1/2, L(s, χ) ≠ 0 unless χ is
principal and s = 1. For the trivial character this is the Riemann hypothesis for the Riemann zeta
function, Mathlib's `RiemannHypothesis`.

Each item is a section of a roadmap that the goal needs, directly or through other items, or a layer
that has no sections; a need of a layer that has sections is a need of each of its sections.
`needs:` names every item it depends on: what its *Needs:* or *Prerequisites:* lines name or, for a
section that has none, what the Dependencies subsection of its layer cites; for a layer, also what
its own Dependencies subsection names; a reference that a negation governs, or that is named as a
consumer, creates no need. `serves:` names the roadmaps under TauCetiRoadmap/ShortGRH/ whose units
need the item directly. An item is done when its targets are in the library. Its slug is the
roadmap's name and the id its heading starts with; other ids of the heading follow the id in
brackets. The area ShortGRH comes last.

This file is generated by `.github/scripts/gen_umbrella.py` from the roadmaps' READMEs. Everything
is regenerated except an item's mark and the clauses the generator does not write, which are kept by
slug; the worker detects completed items from merged pull requests, and a tick records one in the
file. It is regenerated once a day, and at once when the umbrella's own files or the generator
change. A line starting with `>` records a dependency dropped to break a cycle.
